# JEE Main Mathematics - Complex Number

## Topic 11: Geometry of Complex Number

**Total Questions:** 61

### Q1. JEE Main 2025 (7 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If the locus of $z \in \mathrm{C}$, such that
$\operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{z-1}{2 z+\mathrm{i}}\right)+\operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{\bar{z}-1}{2 \bar{z}-\mathrm{i}}\right)=2$
is a circle of radius $r$ and center $(a, b)$ then $\frac{15 a b}{r^2}$ is equal to :

**Options:**

- **(A)** 24
- **(B)** 12
- **(C)** 18
- **(D)** 16

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 18**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned}
& \operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{z-1}{2 z+i}\right)+\operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{\bar{z}-1}{2 \bar{z}-i}\right)=2 \\
& \text { Here, } \frac{z-1}{2 z+i}=\left(\frac{\overline{\bar{z}-1}}{2 \bar{z}-i}\right)=2 \\
& =\operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{z-1}{2 z+i}\right)+\operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{\mathrm{z}-1}{2 z+i}\right)=2 \\
& =2 \operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{z-1}{2 z+1}\right)=2 \Rightarrow \operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{z-1}{2 z+i}\right)=1
\end{aligned}$
Let $z=x+i y$
$\begin{aligned}
& \operatorname{Re}\left(\frac{(x-1)+i y}{2 x+i(2 y+1)}\right)=1 \Rightarrow \operatorname{Re}\left[\frac{((x-1)+i y)(2 x-i(y+1)}{(2 x+i(2 y+1)(2 x-i(2 y+1))}\right]=1 \\
& \Rightarrow \frac{2 x(x-1)+y(2 y+1)}{4 x^2+(2 y+1)^2}=1 \\
& \Rightarrow 2 x^2-2 x+2 y^2+y=4 x^2+4 y^2+1+4 y \\
& \Rightarrow 2 x^2+2 y^2+3 y+2 x+1=0 \\
& \Rightarrow x^2+y^2+x+\frac{3}{2} y+\frac{1}{2}=0 \\
& \text { centre }=\left(\frac{-1}{2}, \frac{-3}{4}\right), r=\sqrt{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{9}{16}-\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{\sqrt{5}}{4} \\
& a=\frac{-1}{2}, b=\frac{-3}{4}, r^2=\frac{5}{16} \\
& 15 \frac{a b}{r^2}=15 \times\left(\frac{-1}{2}\right) \times\left(\frac{-3}{4}\right) \times \frac{16}{5}=18
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $z_1, z_2, z_3 \in C$ are the vertices of an equilateral triangle, whose centroid is $\mathrm{z}_0$, then $\sum_{\mathrm{k}=1}^3\left(\mathrm{z}_{\mathrm{k}}-\mathrm{z}_0\right)^2$ is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0$
- **(B)** $1$
- **(C)** i
- **(D)** $-\mathrm{i}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{z}_1+\mathrm{z}_2+\mathrm{z}_3=3 \mathrm{z}_0 \\ & \left(\mathrm{z}_1+\mathrm{z}_2+\mathrm{z}_3\right)^2=9 \mathrm{z}_0^2 \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{z}_1^2+\mathrm{z}_2^2+\mathrm{z}_3^2+2\left(\mathrm{z}_1^2+\mathrm{z}_2^2+\mathrm{z}_3^2\right)=9 \mathrm{z}_0^2 \\ & \Rightarrow \mathrm{z}_1^2+\mathrm{z}_2^2+\mathrm{z}_3^2=3 \mathrm{z}_6^2\end{aligned}$
$\begin{aligned} & \sum_{\mathrm{k}=1}^3\left(\mathrm{z}_{\mathrm{k}}-\mathrm{z}_0\right)^2=\left(\mathrm{z}_1-\mathrm{z}_0\right)^2+\left(\mathrm{z}_2-\mathrm{z}_0\right)^2+\left(\mathrm{z}_3-\mathrm{z}_0\right)^2 \\ & \quad=\mathrm{z}_1^2+\mathrm{z}_2^2+\mathrm{z}_3^2+3 \mathrm{z}_0^2-2\left(\mathrm{z}_1+\mathrm{z}_2+\mathrm{z}_3\right) \mathrm{z}_0 \\ & =6 \mathrm{z}_0^2-6 \mathrm{z}_0^2 \\ & =0\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let $z$ be a complex number such that $|z|=1$. If $\frac{2+\mathrm{k}^2 \mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{k}+\overline{\mathrm{z}}}=\mathrm{kz}, \mathrm{k} \in \mathbf{R}$, then the maximum distance of $\mathrm{k}+\mathrm{ik}^2$ from the circle $|\mathrm{z}-(1+2 \mathrm{i})|=1$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{5}+1$
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** 3
- **(D)** $\sqrt{3}+1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\sqrt{5}+1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{2+\mathrm{k}^2 \mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{k}+\overline{\mathrm{z}}}=\mathrm{kz} \\ & |\mathrm{z}|^2 \mathrm{k}=2 \\ & \mathrm{k}=2\end{aligned}$
point $\mathrm{p}(2,4)$; center $(1,2)$
distance from circle
$(x-1)^2+(y-2)^2=1$ is max.
if $(\mathrm{OP}+\mathrm{r})=\sqrt{1+4}+1=\sqrt{5}+1$

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let \(\left|z_1-8-2 i\right| \leq 1\) and \(\left|z_2-2+6 i\right| \leq 2, z_1, z_2 \in \mathbf{C}\). Then the minimum value of \(\left|z_1-z_2\right|\) is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** 13
- **(B)** 10
- **(C)** 3
- **(D)** 7

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 7**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/evG9zTEPo_zozWYLLHXKVUkGSnK9sZf_AhSVtZJcMoY.original.fullsize.png">




$\begin{aligned} & \because \mathrm{AB}=\sqrt{100}=10 \\ & \therefore\left|\mathrm{Z}_1-\mathrm{Z}_2\right|_{\min }=10-2-1=7\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2025 (23 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let $\left|\frac{\bar{z}-i}{2 \bar{z}+i}\right|=\frac{1}{3}, z \in C$, be the equation of a circle with center at $C$. If the area of the triangle, whose vertices are at the points $(0,0), \mathrm{C}$ and $(\alpha, 0)$ is 11 square units, then $\alpha^2$ equals:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $50$
- **(B)** $100$
- **(C)** $\frac{81}{25}$
- **(D)** $\frac{121}{25}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $100$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $z=x+i y \Rightarrow \bar{z}=x-i y$
$\begin{aligned}
& 3|\bar{z}-i|=1|2 \bar{z}+i| \\
& =3 \mid(x-(y+1) i|=|2 x+i(1-2 y)| \\
& =3 \sqrt{x^2+(y+1)^2}=\sqrt{(2 x)^2+(1-2 y)^2} \\
& =9\left(x^2+y^2+2 y+1\right)=4 x^2+4 y^2-4 y+1 \\
& \Rightarrow 5 x^2+5 y^2+22 y+8=0 \\
& \Rightarrow \text { Centre } \equiv\left(0,-\frac{11}{5}\right)
\end{aligned}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ov5I9A9--o5t07Qli6YN7R1n4I2juRo63UKaz7HyPMQ.original.fullsize.png">

Area of $\Delta$
$=\frac{1}{2}|\alpha|\left|\frac{-11}{5}\right|=11$
$\begin{aligned} & \Rightarrow \quad|\alpha|=10 \\ & \Rightarrow \quad \alpha^2=100\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let the curve $z(1+i)+\bar{z}(1-i)=4, z \in \mathrm{C}$, divide the region $|z-3| \leq 1$ into two parts of areas $\alpha$ and $\beta$. Then $|\alpha-\beta|$ equals :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1+\frac{\pi}{2}$
- **(B)** $1+\frac{\pi}{3}$
- **(C)** $1+\frac{\pi}{6}$
- **(D)** $1+\frac{\pi}{4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1+\frac{\pi}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/RUAQsiBdybpBmPHacna6Oazdcv5gD3NtmZxd7bzKQek.original.fullsize.png">




$\begin{aligned}
& \text { Let } z=x+i y \\
& (x+i y)(1+i)+(x-i y)(1-i)=4 \\
& x+i x+i y-y+x-i x-i y-y=4 \\
& 2 x-2 y=4 \\
& x-y=2 \\
& |z-3| \leq 1 \\
& (x-3)^2+y^2 \leq 1
\end{aligned}$

Area of shaded region $=\frac{\pi \cdot 1^2}{4}-\frac{1}{2} \cdot 1 \cdot 1=\frac{\pi}{4}-\frac{1}{2}$
Area of unshaded region inside the circle
$=\frac{3}{4} \pi \cdot 1^2+\frac{1}{2} \cdot 1 \cdot 1=\frac{3 \pi}{4}+\frac{1}{2}$
$\therefore$ difference of area $=\left(\frac{3 \pi}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\right)-\left(\frac{\pi}{4}-\frac{1}{2}\right)$
$=\frac{\pi}{2}+1$

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2024 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If $z_1, z_2$ are two distinct complex number such that $\left|\frac{z_1-2 z_2}{\frac{1}{2}-z_1 \bar{z}_2}\right|=2$, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $z_1$ lies on a circle of radius $\frac{1}{2}$ and $z_2$ lies on a circle of radius 1 .
- **(B)** both $z_1$ and $z_2$ lie on the same circle. both $z_1$ and $z_2$ lie on the same circle.
- **(C)** either $z_1$ lies on a circle of radius $\frac{1}{2}$ or $z_2$ lies on a circle of radius 1 .
- **(D)** either $z_1$ lies on a circle of radius 1 or $z_2$ lies on a circle of radius $\frac{1}{2}$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) either $z_1$ lies on a circle of radius 1 or $z_2$ lies on a circle of radius $\frac{1}{2}$.**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{z_1-2 z_2}{\frac{1}{2}-z_1 \bar{z}_2} \times \frac{\bar{z}_1-2 \bar{z}_2}{\frac{1}{2}-\bar{z}_1 z_2}=4 \\ & \left|z_1\right|^2 2 z_1 \bar{z}_2-2 \bar{z}_1 z_2+4\left|z_2\right|^2 \\ & =4\left(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\bar{z}_1 z_2}{2}-\frac{z_1 \bar{z}_2}{2}+\left|z_1\right|^2\left|z_2\right|^2\right)\end{aligned}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/XdhdhRrK1fT55MuNvKItQzeNjzOhL2Gxb43FDC68br8.original.fullsize.png">
$\begin{aligned} & \left(z, \bar{z}_1-1\right)\left(1-2 z_2 \cdot 2 \bar{z}_2\right)=0 \\ & \left(\left|z_1\right|^2-1\right)\left(\left|2 z_2\right|^2-1\right)=0\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>z</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8477;</mi><mo>.</mo></math> . Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> be the circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> in the first quadrant touching the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo></math>axis. If the curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> intersects <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>,</mo></math> then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></math> is equal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **24**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>z</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8477;</mi><mo>.</mo></math> .</p><p>And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>,</p><p>So, taking <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math></p><p>We get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>i</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo mathvariant="italic">Re</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="italic">w</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>And, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo mathvariant="italic">Im</mo><mo mathvariant="italic">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> circle is touching <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo></math>axis, with radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>, so we have equation of circle</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>So, on comparing with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo mathvariant="italic">Im</mo><mo mathvariant="italic">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>And given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> intersects the circle,</p><p>So, solving <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> we get,</p><p>Intersecting point as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>14</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Hence, by distance formula we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>14</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>24</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2023 (12 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> be the circle in the complex plane with centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>. Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></math> and the complex number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> be outside circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> such that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> are collinear, then the smaller value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math> is equal to</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>13</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> be outside circle, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math></p><p>Now given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> are collinear,</p><p>So, by concept of rotation we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mn>0</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>i</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced open="|" close="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>i</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>13</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Hence, the smaller value will be, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="|" close="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p>

<p>For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8450;</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>, if <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></msqrt></math> is the radius of the circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#946;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>,</mo></math> then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>&#946;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is equal to _____.</p>

xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="|" ### Q13. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the center and radius of the circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> are respectively <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>11</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math>, then we have</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msqrt><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>24</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>36</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>32</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>20</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>32</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>And,</p><p><math ### Q14. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>For all <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> on the curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>, let the locus of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mfrac></math> be the curve <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. Then</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** the curves <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>intersect at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> points
- **(B)** the curves <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> lies inside <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** the curves <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> intersect at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> points
- **(D)** the curves <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> lies inside <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) the curves <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>intersect at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> points**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>z</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math> is a circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn></math>.</p><p>Now, let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow></msup></math>, </p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>z</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow></msup><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mn>4</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced open="{" close="}"><mrow><mtext>taking&#160;</mtext><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>z</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now on comparing both side we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>17</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math></p><p>Now get,</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>17</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mstyle displaystyle="true"><msup><mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mstyle></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Which is a equation of ellipse,</p><p><img xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> intersect at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math> points.</p>

### Q16. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></math> be a complex number such that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></math>. Then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> lies on the circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> and centre

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given equation is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></math></p><p>On simplifying the equation, we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>-</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>(</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now putting the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math> we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>On comparing the above equation with the general equation of the circle i.e</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>h</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>We get, centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p>

- **(C)** straight line with the sum of its intercepts on the coordinate axes equals <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>18</mn></math>

### Q18. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="{" close="}"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>:</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math>.  Then the set of all values of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>, for which <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>S</mi></math> for some <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8477;</mi></math>, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now solving <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced></math> put <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math> we get, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn></math> so plotting we get,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/924c118f-9058-4099-9f23-f896255d8fd6-Capture.PNG" style="width: 200px; height: 149px;" /></p><p>Now solving <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>2</mn></math> we get, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>4</mn></math> on plotting the diagram we get,</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b0a0c222-779d-4770-b490-97b4ca7fcb50-Capture1.PNG" style="width: 220px; height: 139px;" /></p><p>Now solving <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now combine diagram of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn></math> will be,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/23f41058-595a-47a6-bc76-7b2f5099d853-Capture.PNG" style="width: 200px; height: 215px;" /></p><p>Now combining all diagram we get,</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1ba02c8c-baf0-4a3f-abc4-013fd97113fa-Capture1.PNG" style="width: 250px; height: 320px;" /></p><p>Hence</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>S</mi></math></p><p>Now for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>w</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>S</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Now <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi></math> putting the value in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mfenced close="]"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p></blockquote>

xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> Either <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>i.e. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> lies on imaginary axis or it lies on the line segment joining <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/cfa52ac5-9902-4fd6-99c7-2e86e16274ed-image.png" style="width: 315px; height: 200px;" /></p><p>So, the minimum distance between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> will be the distance between the points xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>min</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p></blockquote>

### Q20. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math> satisfies <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math></p><p>Also, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Now, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> by solving equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Hence, only <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is true, as point only satisfy this equation.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q21. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> be the origin and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> be the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>Re</mo><mfenced><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, such that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> is a right angled isosceles triangle with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi><mi>B</mi></math> as hypotenuse, then which of the following is NOT true?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>arg</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>arg</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>10</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> be the origin and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> be the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>Re</mo><mfenced><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>,</p><p>Also <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> is a right angled isosceles triangle with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi><mi>B</mi></math> as hypotenuse,</p><p>Plotting the diagram we get, </p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8dfa9ff6-d8e1-4a10-a7b3-060f141caa26-image.png" style="width: 284px; height: 250px;" /></p><p>Now by using concept of rotation we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><msup><mi>e</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msup><mi>e</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi><mi>r</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></math></p><p>Now finding <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>arg</mi><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mn>3</mn></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mn>1</mn></mfenced></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>Now <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>10</mn></msqrt></math></p><p>Now solving <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>arg</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Now solving <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>10</mn></msqrt></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math> is not true.</p></blockquote>

### Q22. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>N</mi></math>, let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">n</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>n</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>. Then the number of elements in the set <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>N</mi><mo>:</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">n</mi></msub><mo>&#8745;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">n</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math> represents a circle with center <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>T</mi><mi mathvariant="italic">n</mi></msub><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>n</mi></mfrac></math> represents a circle with center <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>n</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo>&#8745;</mo><msub><mi>T</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#981;</mi></math>, both circles do not intersect each other.</p><p>When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mfrac><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>n</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>i.e. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#62;</mo><mfrac><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>n</mi></mfrac></math>
then possible values of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mfrac><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>n</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#60;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>n</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p>then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> has infinite solutions for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>N</mi></math></p><p>Hence, there are total four values possible.</p></blockquote><p> </p>

</details>

### Q23. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>z</mi></mfenced></math> represent the principal argument of the complex number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math>. The, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> and  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>arg</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>arg</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math> intersect:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Exactly at one point
- **(B)** Exactly at two points
- **(C)** Nowhere
- **(D)** At infinitely many points.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Nowhere**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>z</mi></mfenced></math> represent the principal argument of the complex number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math>.</p><p>The, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> will represent a circle with centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>units</mtext></math></p><p>And  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>arg</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>arg</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math> will represent arc of the circle with centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>, now on plotting the diagram,</p><p> <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/174fea64-68f2-48c7-8ff4-79db2e59c81a-image.png" style="width: 241px; height: 200px;" />    </p><p>We can see they are intersecting nowhere.</p>

</details>

### Q24. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>z</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>. Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> attains minimum and maximum values, respectively, at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>S</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>S</mi></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mfenced><mrow><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#946;</mi></math> are integers, then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi></math> is equal to ______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **26**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8943;</mo><mfenced><mi>i</mi></mfenced></math> represents interior region of a circle with centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Now <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>Puting <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math>, we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mfenced><mi>i</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> be the points on the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and the circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> represents the distance from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> to any point in the region <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a90ce137-3cc0-4687-8ab8-8e17a3497c06-image.png" style="width: 257px; height: 200px;" /></p><p>Here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>17</mn></msqrt><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>13</mn></msqrt></math> </p><p>So <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is the point representing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>D</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> be the point representing <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>m</mi><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>P</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>tan</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>So <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>25</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>5</mn><mfenced><mrow><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>Hence <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

The number of elements in the set {<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>b</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8450;</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8484;</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#60;</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>4</mn></math>} is _____.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **40**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We know that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>r</mi></math> represents a circle with centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math>.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d18ef026-ba0d-49d6-9f8e-a0a7c28f3b49-image.png" style="width: 160px; height: 152px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#60;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>16</mn></math></p><p>The ordered pairs of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> satisfying the above inequality are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>i.e. total <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn></math> elements are present in the given set</p>

</details>

### Q26. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The number of points of intersection <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Clearly, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> represents a circle with centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> units and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math> represents an ellipse with foci <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and vertices are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> .</p><p>Observe that, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> lies on circle and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>17</mn></msqrt><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>6</mn></math>.</p><p>Hence, the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> lies inside the ellipse.</p><p>So, the graphs of the curves are as shown in figure.</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7b144f8d-c6e6-4516-b361-e939c1cf7f89-e99cb7d8-7921-4b34-bc75-b043839cc1d8-4ceae576-e675-4e6b-9c4c-8132f1a37fa1-image(1).png" style="width: 330px; height: 181px;" /></p><p>It is clear from the above diagram, there exist two complex numbers which satisfy both the given curves.</p>
