### Q27. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The area of the polygon, whose vertices are the non-real roots of the equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>i</mi><msup><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>i</mi><msup><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>i</mi><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mi>i</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></math></p><p>i.e., <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> </p><p>When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></math> </p><p>When <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> will be rejected as vertices would be non-real roots.</p><p>So, the vertices will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Hence, area of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q28. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="{" close="}"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>:</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>arg</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>. Then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8745;</mo><mi>B</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** a portion of a circle centred at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> that lies in the second and third quadrants only
- **(B)** a portion of a circle centred at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> that lies in the second quadrant only
- **(C)** an empty set
- **(D)** a portion of a circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math> that lies in the third quadrant only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) a portion of a circle centred at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> that lies in the second quadrant only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Set <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mover><mi>z</mi><mo mathvariant="bold">&#175;</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b3b59eaf-6dc4-4bf7-bf71-ca2a024e2a32-image.png" style="width: 180px; height: 145px;" /></p><p>Set <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>arg</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>arg</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi></mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math>  Centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f5846ea3-a740-46f2-911d-2b98b3cb95a8-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 208px;" /></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8745;</mo><mi>B</mi></math> will represent a portion of a circle centred at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> that lies in the second quadrant only</p>

</details>

### Q29. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let a circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> in complex plane pass through the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>i</mi></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8800;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> is a point on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> such that the line through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is perpendicular to the line through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>arg</mi><mfenced><mi>z</mi></mfenced></math> is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>24</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>24</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dbf9435b-4388-4da7-b025-5daadc542be2-image.png" style="width: 180px; height: 141px;" /></p><p>Slope of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Slope of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>Now, equation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AP</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>is</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> lies on circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>24</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>arg</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>tan</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>24</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo></math>{<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8450;</mi><mo>:</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>24</mn></math>}. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>&#946;</mi></math> is the point in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math> which is closest to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>25</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is equal to ______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **80**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Which represent a circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> and centred at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now simplifying <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>24</mn></math></p><p>We get, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>24</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>24</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>24</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>12</mn></math></p><p>Now plotting the diagram we get,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b2d63a67-5377-4864-bf4e-0cc617285a9a-9722930.png" style="width: 245px; height: 206px;" /></p><p>Minimum distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> from circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>4</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math> will lie along line joining <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> equation line
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math>
Equation of circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mfenced><mi>ii</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>Solving (i) and (ii) we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>12</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>25</mn><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>16</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>So, for minimum distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>12</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>25</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>12</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>16</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>80</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q31. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#10877;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#10877;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="{" close="}"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>:</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>. Then, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** is an empty set
- **(B)** contains exactly two elements
- **(C)** contains exactly three elements
- **(D)** is an infinite set

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) is an infinite set**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> represents two concentric circles with centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> represents a circle with centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math></p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ccd1bdb1-5d7b-4c31-b670-cc9d3572bc35-image.png" style="width: 201px; height: 200px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> represents the region between the concentric circles with centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Set <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> contains all points on minor arc <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></math> of the circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>.</p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> has infinite elements.</p>

</details>

### Q32. JEE Main 2021 (01 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

If for the complex numbers<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi></math> satisfying <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>, the maximum value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>|</mo></math> is attained at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>b</mi></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi></math> is equal to _____ .

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given that: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn></math> which represents a circle with centre at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>.</p><p>Now <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>|</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>|</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>|</mo></math></p><p>As we know that, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>|</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>|</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>|</mo><mo>|</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>|</mo></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>From tha diagram we can see that, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>|</mo></math> is maximum at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></math> because a point on the circle, which has maximum distance from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/eda0939a-d422-40ba-982e-6e104010fcca-image.png" style="width: 327px; height: 250px;" /></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p>

</details>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>arg</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>tan</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math ### Q34. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math> is a circle in the complex plane
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math> contains exactly two elements
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math> contains only one element
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math> is a straight line in the complex plane

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math> is a straight line in the complex plane**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>Then</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>r</mi><mi>g</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mtext>&#160;is</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mtext>&#160;or</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math> is straight line in complex plane.</p>

</details>

### Q35. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 1)

### Q36. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#8450;</mi></math> be the set of all complex numbers. Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>&#8450;</mi><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="}" open="{"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>&#8450;</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>z</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math> Then, the maximum value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math> for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8745;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is equal to :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn></math> Put <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b9891daf-d440-4e41-9efe-bc59164de1e5-image.png" style="width: 359px; height: 280px;" /></p><p><math <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>Gives <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>Let point on circle be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mi>AP</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>sin</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>&#952;</mi></math></p><p><math ### Q37. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> be the set of all complex numbers. Let</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="{" close="}"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Re</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></math> and</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="{" close="}"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Then the number of elements in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8745;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8745;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is equal to</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(D)** Infinite

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math> (Put <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math>)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>8</mn></math> (put <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math>)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>8</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>y</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>So, xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8745;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8745;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p>

### Q38. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 2)

The equation of a circle is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>Re</mo><mfenced><msup><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>Im</mi><mfenced><mi>z</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>Re</mo><mfenced><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math> where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>.</mo></math> A line which passes through the centre of the given circle and the vertex of the parabola, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>-intercept equal to _________.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We have, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>Re</mo><mfenced><msup><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mfenced><mrow><mo>Im</mo><mfenced><mi>z</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>Re</mo><mfenced><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math></p><p>Equation of circle is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Centre: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Parabola : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>Vertex : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Equation of line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo></math>  intercept <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p>

### Q39. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> be the roots of the equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> form an equilateral triangle with origin. Then, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>a</mi></mfenced></math> is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn></math></p><p>If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> are vertices of equilateral triangles</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msup><mi>e</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi>i</mi><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p>squaring both sides</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><msubsup><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><msubsup><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>12</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>a</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q40. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If the equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><msup><mo>|</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mover><mrow><mover><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover></mrow><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> represents a circle where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi></math> are real constants then which of the following condition is correct?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><msup><mo>|</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><msup><mo>|</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>{</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>}</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><msup><mo>|</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><msup><mi>R</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><msup><mo>|</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>{</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>}</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><msup><mo>|</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mover><mrow><mover><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover></mrow><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><msup><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>z</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mrow><mover><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover></mrow><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mrow><mover><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mi>z</mi></mrow><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover></mrow><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mi>z</mi></math></p><p>Then, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>z</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></math>or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>&#945;</mi><mi>a</mi></mfrac><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mover><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mi>a</mi></mfrac><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>d</mi><mi>a</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is the equation of circle. </p><p>Centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;</mi></mrow><mi>a</mi></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>radius,&#160;</mtext><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mover><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover></mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>d</mi><mi>a</mi></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mover><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mfrac><mi>&#945;</mi><mi>a</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>d</mi><mi>a</mi></mfrac><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> for the equation to represent circle.</p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><msup><mo>|</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>{</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>}</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q41. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> be three sets defined as</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>{</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>&#8450;</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>|</mo><mo>&#8804;</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>}</mo><mo>,</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>{</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>&#8450;</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>}</mo></math> and</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>{</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>&#8450;</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>I</mi><mi>m</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>}</mo><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Then, the set <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8745;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8745;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** is a singleton
- **(B)** has exactly two elements
- **(C)** has infinitely many elements
- **(D)** has exactly three elements

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) has infinitely many elements**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We know that the complex number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> satisfying <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>r</mi></math> represents a circle with centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> and radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> units.</p><p>Hence, for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>|</mo><mo>&#8804;</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi></math> lies on and inside the circle
of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math> units and centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo></math> let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math></p><p>Now, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>y</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>And, for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo></math> again let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>,</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Plotting all the inequalities on the graph, we get</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c3baaf5f-a18c-4c55-865f-843c5061760e-a60846ca-993c-418c-bfdb-b8506fdba3e3-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 195px;" /></p><p>Now, the common part is shown in the shaded part, hence, we get infinite points in the shaded region.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8745;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8745;</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> has infinitely many elements.</p>

</details>

### Q42. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The area of the triangle with vertices <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><msup><mo>|</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi><msup><mo>|</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><msup><mo>|</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/aa849378-3f19-4648-bc11-67902f0ffad4-image.png" style="width: 269px; height: 236px;" /></p><p>From the above diagram, it is clear that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi></math> are perpendicular to each other.</p><p>Here, base <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced></math>, height <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, area of the triangle formed by the vertices i.e., </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>base</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>height</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p>Hence, the required area is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q43. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>w</mi></math> be two complex numbers such that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>z</mi><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>Re</mo><mfenced><mi>w</mi></mfenced></math> has minimum value. Then, the minimum value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>N</mi></math> for which <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>w</mi><mi>n</mi></msup></math> is real, is equal to _______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

### Q45. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let the lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math> (here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>) be normal to a circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math>. If the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is tangent to this circle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math>, then its radius is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math ### Q46. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If <math><mi>z</mi></math> is a complex number satisfying <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>I</mi><mi>m</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo></math> then <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> cannot be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>17</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math><msqrt><mn>10</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math><msqrt><mn>7</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math><msqrt><mn>8</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><msqrt><mn>7</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let <math><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math>
i.e. <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>y</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/846c6b61-9c3a-4699-a0b8-58c380a00218.jpeg" />
Using perpendicular distance of line from origin, minimum value of <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
Maximum value of <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>
So, <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> can&rsquo;t be <math><msqrt><mn>7</mn></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

### Q47. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math><mi>z</mi></math> be a complex number such that <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> . Then, the value of <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msqrt><mn>10</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>15</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>From, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>, we get, 
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>24</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
Hence, <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi></mi><msqrt><mn>6</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>25</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mi></mi><msqrt><mfrac><mrow><mn>49</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></msqrt></math>
<math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q48. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The equation <math><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>|</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>|</mo><mo>,</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></msqrt><mo>,</mo></math> represents:

**Options:**

- **(A)** a circle of radius <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** a circle of radius <math><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** the line through the origin with slope <math><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** the line through the origin with slope <math><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) the line through the origin with slope <math><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/e738cb63-d48d-4f4a-b9d3-bdb9d5fea132-image5.png" /></p><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8658;</mo><msqrt><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></math></p><p><math><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi></math>
Hence, the equation represents a line through the origin with slope <math><mn>1</mn></math> .</p>

</details>

### Q49. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> be such that <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo></math> If <math><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo></math> then:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>&#969;</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>I</mi><mi>m</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>&#969;</mi></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>&#969;</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>4</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>I</mi><mi>m</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>&#969;</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>&#969;</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#969;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#969;</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Using the given condition <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6d93483a-6d23-4e29-a9d9-139fafe1e404-image8.png" style="width: 250px; height: 160px;" />
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>5</mn><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>5</mn><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>5</mn><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#60;</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>We know that the locus of a complex number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> satisfying <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is the perpendicular bisector of the line segment joining the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>b</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Hence, the locus of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#969;</mi></math> satisfying <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math> is the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Hence, <math><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>R</mi><mi>e</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>&#969;</mi></mfenced><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q50. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

All the points in the set <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="{" close="}" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></msqrt></math> lie on a

**Options:**

- **(A)** straight line whose slope is <math><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** circle whose radius is <math><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** circle whose radius is <math><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** straight line whose slope is <math><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) circle whose radius is <math><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> , since <math><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|" separators="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msqrt><msup><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><msqrt><msup><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Hence, locus of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></math> is a circle with radius <math><mn>1</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math><msub><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> be two complex numbers satisfying <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>|</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn></math> and <math><mo>|</mo><msub><mrow><mi>z</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>|</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>. Then the minimum value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>We have,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c35a01eb-b6c5-405d-9c98-7b8136b5dade-image.png" style="width: 203px; height: 200px;" /></p><p>Centres of the circles are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>.</p><p>Radius of the circles are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>4</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p>Circles touch each other internally.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mi>min</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> at the point of contact.</p></blockquote>

</details>
