### Q52. JEE Main 2016 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The point represented by <math><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></math> in the Argand plane moves <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> unit eastwards, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> units northwards and finally from there <math><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math> units in the south-west wards direction. Then its new position in the Argand plane is at the point represented by :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/533d14b5-7074-4dd0-906f-7d669b48be50-image.png" style="width: 281px; height: 200px;" /></p><p>Let <math><mi>P</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#38;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#160;S(z)</mi></math>
By Rotation theorem, rotating about <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>, we get

<math><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>i</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8658;&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q53. JEE Main 2015 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The largest value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math>, for which the region represented by the set <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="}" open="{" separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#969;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is contained in the region represented by the set <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="}" open="{" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math>, is equal to :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>17</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8804;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
Putting <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>i</mi><mi>y</mi></math> and simplifying, we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
The region in shown shaded right side of the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>


<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a00b595e-c89e-4a56-a480-1118f285b2a1-image.png" style="width: 250px; height: 227px;" />
The largest value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> would be the length of perpendicular from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>, on the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2015 (04 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A complex number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> is said to be unimodular if <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>. Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> are complex numbers such that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><menclose notation="top"><mi>z</mi></menclose><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math> is unimodular and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is not unimodular, then the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> lies on a

**Options:**

- **(A)** circle of radius <math> <mrow> <msqrt> <mn>2</mn> </msqrt> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** straight line parallel to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>-axis
- **(C)** straight line parallel to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>-axis
- **(D)** circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>  is unimodular. </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

Squaring both the sides, we get,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mn>2z</mn><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#8757;&#160;</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>z</mi><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>1&#160;</mn></msub><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1&#160;</mn></msub><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1&#160;</mn></msub><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>1&#160;</mn></msub><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2&#160;</mn></msub><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>z</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2&#160;</mn></msup><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2&#160;</mn></msup><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><msup><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> or <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p> Given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is not unimodular

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;&#160;&#160;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math>  Point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> lies on a circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q55. JEE Main 2014 (06 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> is a complex number such that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>,</mi></math> then the minimum value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Is strictly greater than <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** Is strictly greater than <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> but less than <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** Is equal to <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** Lies in the interval <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Lies in the interval <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>z</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>e</mi><mo>.,&#160;&#160;</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>2</mn></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math> The distance of &#39;<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math>&#39; from origin is greater than two.
We have to find minimum of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo></math> distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>.

On the argand plane,

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/af97b440-4a2c-401c-89e2-9cad247aa5a0-image.png" style="width: 378px; height: 229px;" />

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> lies in the dotted regions <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>II</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>III</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>IV</mi><mo>.</mo></math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8756;</mo> </mrow> </math>   The minimum distance is from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math><math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#8712;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2012 (12 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The area of the triangle whose vertices are complex numbers $z, i z, z+i z$ in the Argand diagram is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2|z|^2$
- **(B)** $1 / 2|z|^2$
- **(C)** $4|z|^2$
- **(D)** $|z|^2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1 / 2|z|^2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Vertices of triangle in complex form is $z, i z, z+i z$
In cartesian form vertices are $(x, y),(-y, x)$ and $(x-y, x+y)$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore \text { Area of triangle }=\frac{1}{2}\left|\begin{array}{ccc}
x & y & 1 \\
-y & x & 1 \\
x-y & x+y & 1
\end{array}\right| \\
& =\frac{1}{2}[x(x-x-y)-y(-y-x+y)+1 \\
& \left.\left(-y x-y^2-x^2+x y\right)\right]
\end{aligned}
$$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& =\frac{1}{2}\left[-x y+x y-y^2-x^2\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(x^2+y^2\right) \\
& (\because \text { Area can not be negative }) \\
& =\frac{1}{2}|z|^2 \quad\left(\because z=x+i y,|z|^2=x^2+y^2\right)
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q57. JEE Main 2012 (Offline)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If $z \neq 1$ and $\frac{z^2}{z-1}$ is real, then the point represented by the complex number $z$ lies

**Options:**

- **(A)** either on the real axis or on a circle passing through the origin
- **(B)** on a circle with centre at the origin
- **(C)** either on the real axis or on a circle not passing through the origin
- **(D)** on the imaginary axis

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) either on the real axis or on a circle passing through the origin**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $z=x+$ iy $(\because x \neq 1$ as $z \neq 1)$
$z^2=\left(x^2-y^2\right)+i(2 x y)$
$\frac{z^2}{z-1}$ is real 
$\Rightarrow$ its imaginary part $=0$
$\Rightarrow 2 x y(x-1)-y\left(x^2-y^2\right)=0$
$\Rightarrow y\left(x^2+y^2-2 x\right)=0$
$\Rightarrow y=0 ; x^2+y^2-2 x=0$
$\therefore \mathrm{z}$ lies either on real axis or on a circle through origin.

</details>

### Q58. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $|z+4| \leq 3$, then the maximum value of $|z+1|$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4$
- **(B)** $10$
- **(C)** $6$
- **(D)** $0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $6$**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the Argand diagram maximum value of $|z+1|$ is $6$.
Alternative:
$\begin{aligned}
& |z+1|=|z+4-3| \\
& \leq|z+4|+|-3|=6.
\end{aligned}$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/dz7KrD-F6YZoUBJzY_drwRdfX5wnYq4IW0tn27VZ3rk.original.fullsize.png">

</details>

### Q59. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If $\left|z^2-1\right|=|z|^2+1$, then $z$ lies on

**Options:**

- **(A)** the real axis
- **(B)** an ellipse
- **(C)** a circle
- **(D)** the imaginary axis.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) the imaginary axis.**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\left|z^2-1\right|^2=\left(|z|^2+1\right) \Rightarrow\left(z^2-1\right)\left(\bar{z}^2-1\right)=|z|^4+2|z|^2+1$
$\Rightarrow z^2+\bar{z}^2+2 z \bar{z}=0 \Rightarrow z+\bar{z}=0$
$\Rightarrow R(z)=0 \Rightarrow z$ lies on the imaginary axis.

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let $Z_1$ and $Z_2$ be two roots of the equation $x^2+a Z+b=0$ being complex. Further, assume that the origin, $Z_1$ and $\mathrm{Z}_2$ form an equilateral triangle. Then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $a^2=4 b$
- **(B)** $a^2=b$
- **(C)** $a^2=2 b$
- **(D)** $a^2=3 b$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $a^2=3 b$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{z}^2+\mathrm{az}+\mathrm{b}=0 ; \mathrm{z}_1+\mathrm{z}_2=-\mathrm{a} \& \mathrm{z}_1 \mathrm{z}_2=\mathrm{b}$
$0, \mathrm{z}, \mathrm{z}_2$ form an equilateral $\Delta$
$\therefore 0^2+\mathrm{z}_1{ }^2+\mathrm{z}_2{ }^2=0 . \mathrm{z}_1+\mathrm{z}_1 \cdot \mathrm{z}_2+\mathrm{z}_2 \cdot 0$
(for equation $\Delta, \mathrm{z}_1{ }^2+\mathrm{z}_2{ }^2+\mathrm{z}_3{ }^2=\mathrm{z}_1 \mathrm{z}_2+\mathrm{z}_2 \mathrm{z}_3+\mathrm{z}_3 \mathrm{z}_1$ ) $z_1^2+z_2^2=z_1 z_2$ or $\left(z_1+z_2\right)^2=3 z_1 z_2$
$\therefore \mathrm{a}^2=3 \mathrm{~b} \text {. }$

</details>
